## 4. Shared naming and implementation rules supplied by the student

- Main IDs: `userId`, `moderatorId`, `airlineId`, `airportId`, `flightId`, `cabinId`, `flightBookingId`, `passengerId`, `hotelId`, `roomId`, `hotelBookingId`, `reviewId`, `paymentId`, `refundId`, `itineraryId`, `dayId`, `activityId`, `budgetId`, `postId`, `commentId`, `reportId`, `notificationId`. Do not substitute `user_id`, `flightID`, or random ownership names.
- One shared role enum: `USER`, `ADMIN`, `AIRLINE_MODERATOR`, `HOTEL_MODERATOR`. Account statuses: `ACTIVE`, `SUSPENDED`, `DEACTIVATED`. Temporary login lockout is separate from suspension.
- Booking statuses: `PENDING`, `CONFIRMED`, `CANCELLED`; add any officially required missing status once through team agreement. Payment: `PENDING`, `SUCCEEDED`, `FAILED`, `REFUNDED`. Refund: `PENDING`, `SUCCEEDED`, `FAILED`. Blog post: `DRAFT`, `PUBLISHED`. These are supplied agreements; this log does not silently resolve workbook/status-mapping gaps or implement other modules' enums.
- Entity names: `User`, `ModeratorAssignment`, `Airline`, `Airport`, `Flight`, `CabinInventory`, `FlightBooking`, `Passenger`, `Hotel`, `Room`, `RoomAvailability`, `HotelBooking`, `Review`, `Payment`, `Refund`, `Itinerary`, `ItineraryDay`, `Activity`, `BudgetEntry`, `TransportCost`, `ExchangeRate`, `BlogPost`, `Comment`, `Like`, `Tag`, `Report`, `Notification`, `AuditLog`.
- Main MongoDB documents use `_id`, `createdAt`, `updatedAt`; prefer Mongoose timestamps. Normal-user ownership uses `userId`, for example on bookings, itineraries, posts, comments, and reviews. Public user identity exposes `_id` as `userId` rather than storing a duplicate ID.
- DTOs: `CreateXDto`, `UpdateXDto`; specific actions use `RegisterDto`, `LoginDto`, `CreateFlightBookingDto`, `CreateHotelBookingDto`, `CreatePaymentDto`. NestJS classes use names such as `FlightsService`/`FlightsController`, `HotelsService`/`HotelsController`, `PaymentsService`/`PaymentsController`, `ItinerariesService`/`ItinerariesController`.
- All API routes use `/api`; examples include `/api/auth/login`, `/api/flights/search`, `/api/flight-bookings`, `/api/hotels/search`, `/api/hotel-bookings`, `/api/payments`, `/api/itineraries`, `/api/posts`. Swagger currently uses `/api/docs` and `/api/docs-json`.
- Agreed environment names only: `MONGODB_URI`, `JWT_SECRET`, `STRIPE_SECRET_KEY`, `STRIPE_WEBHOOK_SECRET`, `FXRATES_API_KEY`, `PORT`. Agree on new names before adding them. Values stay in ignored local `.env` files; commit safe names/placeholders in `.env.example` only.
- Controllers handle HTTP; services handle business rules; models/data access handle persistence. Each owner supplies schema, DTOs, validation, service, controller, authorization, ownership checks, errors, Swagger and meaningful testing for their feature.
- Reuse Person 1's future authentication/role guards, Flights search, Hotels search and Payments logic rather than copying them. A guard alone does not prove resource ownership. Do not accept a client-supplied user/provider ID as authorization.
- Agree interfaces before dependencies: Persons 3/4 Flight and Cabin; Persons 5/6 Room and Availability; Persons 4/6/8 bookings/payments. Do not edit another owner's module or a shared schema without coordination. Shared changes follow: ask → agree → change → inform affected owners → synchronize.

## 5. Team Git, AI, privacy, and review rules

1. Work on the currently authorized step only. Analyze supplied documents as evidence; document examples do not authorize unrelated setup, deployment, or code. Inspect the requirement, accepted interfaces, existing implementation, repository root, remote, branch, and working tree before changes.
2. Preserve existing and unrelated work. Do not automatically reset, stash, discard, or overwrite it. Fetch current refs at each development task. Keep local `main` aligned with `origin/main` using a safe fast-forward-only update; report divergence instead of forcing it. Avoid switching the working files merely to refresh that ref. If fetching or base verification fails, record the failure, do not claim refs are current, and hold dependent edits until the current base is verified.
3. Never develop directly on `main` or `develop`. Start from current `develop`; use `feature/<requirement-id>-<description>`, `fix/<requirement-id>-<description>`, `docs/<description>`, or `refactor/<description>`. Continue an existing suitable feature/PR branch for revisions to the same work.
4. Merge current `develop` into the task branch regularly and before opening/updating its PR. Never force-push, including force-with-lease; do not rewrite published history. `main` holds stable milestones: updating it from `develop` requires a separately reviewed release PR, not a direct push.
5. Review the full intended diff, stage intended files only, and commit with meaningful messages such as `feat(auth): implement login`, `fix(hotels): prevent overlapping bookings`, or `docs(workflow): consolidate student record`. **Latest student instruction, 2026-11-04: batch multiple requirements and wait until the student explicitly says the session is done before opening a new session PR or performing final review/merge.** Continue existing PR #10 as a draft; do not create per-requirement PRs. This supersedes the earlier one-PR-per-requirement preference for this session. Keep each requirement's implementation, tests, Swagger, documentation and continuous-log updates together in meaningful commits/checkpoints on the session branch. Create the session PR into `develop` when the session's changes are ready, describing behavior, IDs, actual verification, AI assistance, limitations, integration commits and this file's dated entries. Standalone documentation work is not counted as a completed functional requirement.
6. Implementation authorization, a passing test suite, AI review, student understanding, and final human PR approval are separate facts. Start each entry's final human review as PENDING. The responsible student reviews/understands their work and the team leader reviews each PR; resolve important review comments before merging. Record the actual reviewer and evidence only when confirmed. Do not automatically merge a PR.
7. Each student owns one continuous log. Update it at planning, implementation, verification, and review checkpoints. Preserve historical failures/results; append corrections. This authorized consolidation may reword old entries while retaining their important substance. Do not create separate prompt/policy/template files for this workflow.
8. Record real actions and concise reasons; distinguish student from AI work and who accepted, modified, or rejected suggestions. Do not invent timestamps, prompts, approvals, checks, contributions, model names, commits, PRs, or learning. Use NOT PROVIDED, NOT DISCLOSED, PENDING, or NOT RUN where appropriate. Label reconstructed entries RETROSPECTIVE with their evidence and creation date.
9. Never paste credentials, full connection strings, passwords, password hashes, tokens, cookies, or private user records into logs, prompts, screenshots, outputs, or commits. Do not ask teammates to paste secrets. Use sanitized diagnostic outcomes. Pattern scans supplement human review; they do not prove the absence of every possible secret.
10. Prefer an isolated local test database. Shared Atlas is for the agreed application connection; do not wipe shared collections or alter another student's records. Agree isolated records/cleanup before tests that need shared data.
11. Test in proportion to the change. Backend work needs build, valid/invalid cases, authorization/ownership, persistence, and Swagger as applicable. Documentation work needs links, consistency, scope/privacy and diff checks. Report backend tests NOT RUN when code is unchanged rather than recycling historical PASS results as new tests.
12. Use AI as a tutor and critic: plan, inspect small changes, explain the diff, verify independently, and retain decisions. Students do their own required first drafts and no-AI exercises. Never certify understanding from AI output alone.

## 6. Minimum information in each dated entry

These 14 information groups are required; they can be combined into readable paragraphs/tables rather than 14 separate files or repeated templates:

1. Date/timezone, student ID/name if supplied, role/module, task and scope.
2. Requirement IDs or policy/source references; branch and issue if one exists.
3. AI tool/model if known, or explicitly no AI; do not guess.
4. Starting state and materials/code actually inspected.
5. Plan, design critique, authorization status, and evidence for actual approvals.
6. Chronological observable actions with student/AI attribution.
7. Decisions, alternatives, concise reasons, and who decided.
8. Important sanitized prompts/context and how outputs were used.
9. AI output accepted, modified, rejected or pending, by whom and why.
10. Changed files and purpose.
11. Checks: expected behavior, observed result, PASS/FAIL/NOT RUN; identify historical versus current checks.
12. Failures, corrections, retests, assumptions, limitations and remaining work.
13. Actual commit/PR references when available; do not invent future references.
14. Student explanation/learning, final human-review status, reviewer and evidence when confirmed.

Each feature, fix, or meaningful documentation PR includes its responsible student's log update. Several contributors link their own entries. Record checkpoints during the work and update actual publication/review references when they exist.

The student supplied the Person 1 assignment, team naming/Git rules, project PDF and reviewed workbook, and asked whether to use web/app or IDE Codex. AI recommended working with the VSCode repository context. That advice is historical; it is not a new product recommendation in this record.

Plan and actual use: inspect the repository and authoritative requirements; identify auth rules, ownership boundaries, status-enum gaps and cross-module decisions; propose a shared contract before implementing. AI prepared `docs/requirements/authentication-contract-proposal.md` and documented its use in the then-required prompt log. No authentication code was generated/integrated at the analysis stage; local secret values were not read.

Key choices: reuse one role/status definition; use `_id` internally and `userId` publicly; normalize email; prevent privileged self-registration; distinguish suspension from failed-login lockout; defer final session and provider interfaces to the relevant features. Verification/evidence: PR #1 was reviewed and merged with explicit student approval; no runtime feature or test result is claimed for this contract-only stage. No detailed contemporaneous command transcript beyond retained evidence is reconstructed. Files: accepted contract and historical prompt record. Remaining: registration implementation and deferred interface decisions.

### RETROSPECTIVE — 2026-11-01–02: ACC-01 registration through PR #2

Reconstructed on 2026-11-03 from the conversation, previous prompt record, backend and PR history. Scope: ACC-01 and relevant GEN-13 validation/hash rules. Branch: `feature/ACC-01-user-registration`; issue: none supplied. Authorization: the student's quoted agreement above; subsequent explicit PR approval was provided separately.

Starting state/materials: the accepted User contract and reviewed registration rules existed; the first backend slice still needed implementation. AI inspected the repository, implemented NestJS setup and registration in small connected pieces, documented setup/request flow/manual checks, and verified it. The student supplied decisions, reviewed PRs and handled local Atlas setup; this record does not attribute AI-written code to the student as manually authored code.

| Implementation and AI decision | Reason / disposition |
| --- | --- |
| User schema plus shared role/account-status enums; public registration fixes USER/ACTIVE | Implements agreed naming and prevents caller-selected privilege/status. AI implementation; integrated through the approved PR. |
| DTO validation, literal-true terms/privacy consent, compliant password, normalized email | Reject invalid requests and enforce consent; normalization makes uniqueness consistent. |
| Database unique index and duplicate-key handling | Validation/pre-check alone cannot prevent concurrent duplicate registrations. |
| Asynchronous salted scrypt password hashing | Stores salted hashes without blocking synchronous derivation; never return hash material. Tutorial bcrypt examples did not require replacing this choice. |
| Public response allowlist and hidden sensitive fields | Avoid accidental exposure when the schema grows. |
| Swagger and isolated local MongoDB tests | Make behavior reviewable without modifying the team's Atlas database. |
| TypeScript/tsc-watch rather than retaining a vulnerable CLI dependency path | Retained required build/watch behavior after dependency checks exposed tooling issues. |

Files/purpose: `backend/` application/bootstrap/configuration, User model/shared enums, registration DTO/controller/service/password utility, tests, dependency scripts/lockfile and backend README; safe environment examples/ignore rules; root README and the historical prompt record. Consult PR #2 for the exact file diff rather than treating this summary as a substitute.

Actual tooling at the final registration checkpoint: NestJS 12.3.2, Mongoose 9.11.4, TypeScript 5.9.3 and Node 24. Build uses `tsc -p tsconfig.build.json`; development uses `tsc-watch -p tsconfig.build.json --onSuccess "node dist/main.js"`. Tests use the Node test runner and mongodb-memory-server; an installed Windows MongoDB binary can run a separate temporary instance, otherwise a download is needed.

| Observed failure / correction | Recorded result |
| --- | --- |
| PowerShell blocked `npm.ps1` under execution policy | Used `npm.cmd`. |
| Sandbox network/cache restrictions interrupted dependency operations | Retried necessary installation with appropriate access; did not represent failed attempts as success. |
| CLI dependency paths showed vulnerabilities; an interim build lacked `nest` | Replaced CLI-dependent scripts with tsc/tsc-watch; final build passed. Intermediate audit reports had two then five vulnerabilities; final installation reported zero known vulnerabilities at that time. |
| MongoDB binary download was large and incomplete | Stopped the download after locating an installed MongoDB 8.3.11 binary; used a separate temporary test instance, not a running shared database. |
| A test query did not explicitly select a hidden field | Corrected the test query and reran the suite. |
| Bootstrap failure could leave resources open | Ensured failed startup closes the application. |

Historical verification, **not rerun for the current documentation change**:

| Check | Expected behavior | Observed result | Status |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Production TypeScript build | Compile application | Completed successfully | PASS |
| Automated tests | Registration/configuration behavior and invalid cases work in isolation | All 13 passed: 11 registration integration tests and 2 configuration tests | PASS |
| Compiled startup, registration and Swagger smoke check | Start compiled app, register, publish API documentation | Recorded successful checks with an isolated test database | PASS |
| Dependency installation/audit | Report known dependency issues | Final installation reported zero known vulnerabilities then; this is not a current security guarantee | PASS |
| Shared Atlas and local secret file in automated checks | Remain unused | Isolated tests/smoke checks did not use them | PASS |

Disposition/review: AI-generated implementation and corrections were integrated through the student's explicit PR approval. The student asked whether PR2 was done and then said **"i approve"**. Final PR approval is confirmed for that historical change; independent mastery of every code path is not inferred. Login/session/profile/reset/guards were intentionally deferred, not claimed complete.

### RETROSPECTIVE — 2026-11-01–02: local configuration, saving, and project status

Reconstructed on 2026-11-03 from the conversation and recorded diagnostic outcomes; exact per-action times are not reconstructed. Scope: local startup support, saving work, and progress reporting, not a new feature. Relevant policy: keep secrets local and keep local main current. Issue: none supplied; associated registration PR: #2. Tool: Codex; student handled their own credentials.

The student reported a DNS failure from a placeholder Atlas hostname. AI explained using the actual Atlas hostname, asking only for the non-secret hostname rather than credentials. After the hostname was corrected, the failure changed to database authentication. The student initially edited local configuration without resetting the database password; after clarification they reset the Atlas database user's password and saved the matching local configuration.

Disposition/actions: student supplied the actual hostname and confirmed reset/saving; AI guided diagnostics and later verified a private, read-only connection ping. Only sanitized outcomes were reported. DNS and authentication failures were real failed attempts; the later ping passed. No secret values, full URI, database passwords, tokens or private records are reproduced here. Saved local credentials remained outside Git. No shared application records were modified by the ping.

AI reported registration complete, remaining scope, and the difference between synchronizing main with GitHub and promoting develop through a release PR. These ongoing rules are preserved in section 5.

Learning point: DNS resolution and authentication are different failure stages; an account password must match the Atlas database user, not merely a local edit. Remaining: future application features; no unresolved Atlas connection failure was recorded after the successful ping.

These are AI-provided study prompts tied to actual registration code. They are **not student answers** or certification of understanding. The student should answer in their own words, inspect the diff, and perform any required no-AI exercise themselves.

| --- | --- |
| Trace a registration request from route to response | Bootstrap/global validation, DTO, controller, service, model, dependency injection and public response mapping. |
| Why normalize email, and why is an application duplicate check insufficient? | Trim/lowercase, database unique index, concurrent requests and duplicate-key handling. |
| Why require literal consent and reject unexpected fields? | DTO validation, validation-pipe configuration, caller-supplied role/status rejection. |
| What makes password storage safe here? | Random salt, asynchronous scrypt, hash verification design; never display real hash material in the log. |
| Why use both hidden fields and an output allowlist? | A projection choice can change; response mapping still controls exposure. |
| Which future requirements are not implemented by fields alone? | Roles/status/counters do not provide login, expiry, guards, lockout or authorization without enforcement. |
| What do 201/400/409 responses and the tests demonstrate? | Valid registration, invalid DTOs and duplicate email; identify what tests do not cover yet. |
| How are tests isolated from Atlas and secret files? | Temporary local MongoDB, cleanup, and test/bootstrap setup rather than connecting through the real application environment. |
| Explain the PR diff in two minutes, then make a small change yourself | State the problem, design, validation evidence and limitations; actual student demonstration remains PENDING. |

### Requirements, inspected sources and plan

The reviewed workbook's `Mandatory!A4:F4`, acceptance text `D4`, requires five consecutive failed login attempts to lock the account for 15 minutes **and show a lockout message**; a successful login resets the counter. The accepted contract's short summary omitted the message; AI verified the original row and repaired that summary. The project PDF maps ACC-05 to the existing login endpoint and requires consistent errors, but specifies no exact message or status code.

Inspected: current auth/users/session/controller code and approved User fields, existing constants/test fixture/package scripts, README/contract/log, Git status/remotes/refs, exact ACC-05 source row and relevant PDF sections. MongoDB atomicity/update-pipeline, Mongoose and OWASP authentication documentation were checked. No .env contents, private user data or credentials were needed. Prior 41-test results belong to ACC-04 and are not new ACC-05 results.

Plan: use existing `failedLoginAttempts` and `lockedUntil`; implement atomic database updates, integrate them into credential handling, document the required error behavior, test ordinary/expired/concurrent cases in the existing isolated fixture, update this log, synchronize and open one focused PR into develop. No User schema, enum, environment name, dependency or other person's module changes are needed. Failed-login lockout remains separate from suspension, refresh and logout.

### Decisions, implementation and AI attribution

| Decision / work | Actor and disposition | Reason |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Five failures / 15 minutes / success reset / lockout message | Supplied requirement, implemented by coordinating AI | Follow the exact reviewed acceptance criteria; no new threshold is invented. |
| Ordinary credential error versus required lockout response | Coordinating AI chose HTTP 401 consistently; message becomes `Account temporarily locked. Try again later.` on the fifth failure and during an active lock | The explicit lockout message is required; counters/deadlines are never response fields. Unknown emails keep the ordinary generic error and create no records. |
| Database time and atomic current-state predicates | Coordinating AI proposed; independent AI review ACCEPTED with no material findings | Match current password hash, ACTIVE status and unlocked deadline at the write; avoid stale reads, lost increments or resetting a lock that won first. |
| Failure pipeline | Coordinating AI | Increment the stored streak up to five; an expired prior lock starts a new streak at one. The fifth failure sets the deadline from database `$$NOW`. Active locks match no write, so extra attempts cannot extend them. |
| Successful login | Coordinating AI | Conditional atomic reset to zero/null only when still eligible and unlocked; return fresh public-safe account state. A separate lock read controls error wording only, not access. |
| Correct-password race and existing sessions | Coordinating AI, reviewed independently | The successful reset is the ordering point. If the fifth failure locks first, the stale verifier fails; if success resets first, later failures start a new streak. Already accepted sessions are not revoked by new failed logins. |
| Tests/source verification/security review | Delegated AI assistance; coordinating AI owns integration and final verification | Reuse the isolated Mongo fixture and deterministic barriers; never use Atlas or a local secret file. |

Changed files/purpose at this implementation checkpoint: `auth.constants.ts` for policy constants; UsersService for atomic persistence; AuthService for lock-aware credential decisions; AuthController for Swagger behavior; existing integration tests for acceptance/concurrency/security; backend/root README and accepted contract for behavior; this file for the continuous record. No new workflow file is created. Invalid DTOs do not count as credential attempts; inactive account restrictions and required password changes remain enforced.

### 2026-11-04 — agreed password design and implementation checkpoint

The student answered **"Use this design"** to the shared-field/restricted-cookie proposal. Design agreement is CONFIRMED; final code/PR review remains PENDING. User/AuthSession now add the agreed hidden credentialVersion (default zero, nonnegative safe integer). No new application environment variable or enum is introduced. Independent AI review refined the restricted cookie to use a revocable AuthSession, preserving the approved 15-minute behavior while ensuring logout prevents replay. This refinement adds no separate collection or extra shared field.

Implementation in order: reusable Roles decorator/guard and cross-module test; ACC-07a admin reset and restricted next-login flow; ACC-08 ordinary current-password replacement using the same atomic password-update primitive. Coordinating AI owns AuthController, AuthService integration, PasswordChangeGuard and PasswordManagementService; delegated AI authored User persistence/DTOs, session-purpose/version enforcement, nested validation and API/security tests. Coordinating AI integrates and verifies all outputs; AI code is not attributed to manual student authorship.

API/decisions: ADMIN-only POST /api/admin/users/:userId/reset-password accepts a compliant temporaryPassword for USER/AIRLINE_MODERATOR/HOTEL_MODERATOR accounts, never ADMIN. Any existing target status is preserved; reset does not unsuspend/reactivate accounts. The next eligible login returns only passwordChangeRequired=true with a restricted secure cookie and clears normal cookies. PATCH /api/auth/change-password accepts currentPassword/newPassword for the server-established caller, requires a compliant different replacement, returns 204/clears all cookies/requires fresh login. Restricted tokens permit only password change/logout, carry a distinct signed purpose, and use 15-minute server expiry. A logout revokes the exact restricted session before clearing its cookie.

Concurrency rationale: session creation keeps the version actually verified with the password; it never reloads a new version to stamp stale credentials. Password replacement CAS matches hash/version/flag/ACTIVE status. Admin reset atomically checks target role. Both mutate hash/flag/counters/version on one User document using database time, bounded against unsafe version increments. Guards compare the session's version with live account state and recheck after activity update. Earlier sessions remain logically invalid even when their documents survive until TTL cleanup; no physical deletion/transaction rollback is claimed. Legacy missing stored versions mean zero. A review found a guard comment/implementation mismatch for an explicitly empty restricted cookie; coordinating AI corrected the check to distinguish absent from malformed and require validation of any presented restricted proof.

Dependency limits: the reviewed workbook lists ACC-14 account listing/search/filter as an ACC-07a dependency. That administration module's ownership remains deferred and it is not implemented by this password feature. ACC-18 administrator seeding also remains separate; isolated tests create fixtures, not production administrator accounts. No new notification/provider/payment implementation or change to another person's module is authorized or claimed.

Other correctness repair: PR #8 identified that global nested validation produced empty arrays for passengers. Person 1's bootstrap now traverses child errors into nonempty dotted field paths, preserves the existing error wrapper, omits request values and skips unsafe prototype segments. Five real mini-Nest HTTP tests cover nested/unknown/top-level/privacy behavior without MongoDB or .env.

### Findings and exact owner follow-up

The first four findings below have automated/runtime reproduction. Remaining findings are direct source-versus-workbook gaps, not new passing/failing tests. Missing integration stubs are separated from defects; no source change is claimed as fixed.

| Owner / requirement | Finding and requested correction |
| --- | --- |
| Gamila / FLT-03, FLT-04; shared AppModule | FlightsModule is never imported by any production-reachable module. Both new POST endpoints are absent from Swagger and return 404. Register the module in AppModule (coordinate the shared root edit) and verify routes, guards and valid requests through the assembled app. Source: backend/src/app.module.ts:13-32. |
| Gamila / cabin creation | backend/src/flights/flights.service.ts:157-165 uses ordered insertMany without a transaction. A later unique conflict returns 409 while earlier cabins remain inserted. Make the batch atomic with the existing unique index and appropriate concurrency handling; a precheck alone does not resolve races. Add an integration case for a conflict after a valid first item. |
| Kiki / status lookup failure | Public operations correctly fail closed, but toDto calls attributionNames, which calls the failing status port even for an owner already authorized by canRead. Thus the owner's read also fails with 500, contradicting the advertised contract and its new test. Correct the owner-response/name handling without removing visibility checks or hiding public lookup failures. Source: itineraries.service.ts:639-655; failing expectation at test/itineraries.e2e.test.ts:569. |
| Kiki / ITN-01, ITN-05, GEN-12 | Title validation permits 120 instead of the workbook's 100; MAX_DAYS is 90 instead of 60; country/city validation checks shape/free text rather than membership in the official reference lists. The 101-character/ZZ/61st-day probes all succeeded. Correct create/update validation, day limit and shared reference membership; coordinate reference owners instead of inventing duplicate catalogs. |
| Kiki / ITN-10, ITN-14, ITN-16 | Activity DTO/schema has no optional cost/currency, and budgetSummary sums daily budget entries and overall transport only. Required activity costs cannot contribute to Activities totals. Transport dayId is optional, and day/city subtotals ignore assigned intercity transport. Align activity cost storage, required transport-day association and the workbook's totals/breakdowns. Sources: dto/activity.dto.ts, dto/transport-cost.dto.ts:34-37, itineraries.service.ts:327-395. |
| Kiki / ITN-18 | Rates refresh after one hour rather than once daily; failure rejects cache after seven days although the workbook says use the last cached rates. ### Implementation by Codex under Malak's direction

- UsersService now implements getAccountStatuses(userIds): Promise<Map<string, AccountStatus>> and getDisplayNames(userIds): Promise<Map<string,string>>. Both normalize/deduplicate valid ObjectIds and project only required fields. Missing IDs are omitted; display names exclude deactivated accounts. No credentials/whole User documents or public account-directory endpoint. The agreed status-only contract is preserved. Itinerary ports bind to the exported UsersService instance; temporary direct-collection adapters are removed.
- Owner responses now carry explicit proven owner identity into DTO attribution. They do not depend on another owner-status lookup. On an owned clone's response, unavailable original-author status yields Deleted user rather than an unverified name. Public lookup errors still propagate and private itineraries remain owner-only, including for admins. No authentication/account-status guard is bypassed.
- CurrenciesService exposes/accepts only the seven official Countries.csv codes while preserving unrelated stored rows during setup. This updates the previous initial-set interpretation under the revised PDF, once for both profiles and itinerary use. The auth contract's old ACC-03 ambiguity is resolved by the revised ACC-01 mapping.
- Approved ReferenceDataModule supplies indexed Country/City schemas, one reusable service, documented public GET /api/countries and /api/cities, and npm.cmd run seed:references. Setup performs idempotent setOnInsert writes with no HTTP/booking/auth startup. Application startup initializes indexes but does not insert reference data. No new environment name or live Atlas seed/migration.
- Itinerary title max100, max60 days, official country/city validation and canonical city/country storage. Cities must belong to selected itinerary countries; removing countries cannot strand existing days. Keyword search now includes official country names/codes. Invalid null updates to required itinerary fields are rejected instead of reaching Mongoose as invalid values.
- Activity cost/currency is supported, with non-negative two-decimal amounts and official currency membership; currency defaults to the itinerary currency when cost is first supplied, and stored amounts are not relabeled by later display-preference changes. Costs survive cloning and count once under ACTIVITIES. New transport entries require an owned dayId; day/city totals include assigned transport without double counting overall totals. Deleting a day removes its associated transport entries.
- Legacy compatibility: storage still permits old missing transport dayId; parent reads/edits remain functional. Updating an unassigned transport entry requires explicit reassignment to an owned day. No guessed day allocation or destructive migration. Until reassigned, legacy unassigned amounts remain in the overall transport total without an invented per-day allocation.
- FX cache now uses one successful snapshot per UTC calendar day, coalesces concurrent requests per service instance, and uses the last cached snapshot on provider failure regardless of age. Authenticated summaries use the current supported user preference by default; guests retain itinerary currency and explicit display currency is honored. Provider date/stale flag remain visible; booking/payment amounts are unchanged. Multiple backend processes do not share the in-memory in-flight map; this is not a claim of a distributed provider-call lock.
- Upload acceptance/Swagger now limits images to JPG/PNG. ITN-25 includes room count. ITN-26 now takes departureAirportCode and one-way/round-trip dates, resolves destination airports in the first itinerary city, defaults dates to itinerary start/last day, and validates airports/dates/passenger relationships. It does not invent the former nine-passenger search cap. Existing Flights/Hotels search adapters still return503 until their owners wire services; corrected criteria are tested with doubles, not advertised as live search.
- Existing README/auth/itinerary contract documents describe the final behavior, interfaces and operator setup. Kiki's own student log is not edited or attributed to Malak/Codex. This continuous student file records Malak's cross-module assistance.

- createCabins runs the ordered insertMany batch inside its connection's MongoDB transaction, passing the transaction session explicitly. A later unique-class failure aborts the earlier inserts before mapping the error to409. The annotations now use MongooseSchema.Types.ObjectId, matching the pre-existing TypeScript/shared-contract BSON references and the existing AuthSession pattern. No reference names, collection names, enum values or stored IDs are changed, and no database migration is run. This is correction of runtime annotations to the already intended contract, not a new assignment/schema design.

### Review method and independently confirmed findings

Codex delegated separate read-only reviews of payments/refunds, blog, and hotels. Instructions required concrete triggers, source lines and ownership, no application edits or teammate messages, and separation of known pending interfaces from defects. Root reviewed shared wiring/flight changes and checked findings with short inline probes against freshly compiled production classes. Synthetic Model/Stripe stubs and disconnected Mongoose validation were used where stated; these probes do not claim live provider or end-to-end certification.

- **Omar/Hicham — payment-success race (P1):** `payments.service.ts:225`. After retry replaces intent A with B, concurrent success handlers for A can both read PENDING/B. The losing update uses stale `isOlderIntent` and refunds A even though A won and confirmed the booking. An isolated deterministic probe using actual compiled PaymentsService/RefundsService and a two-reader barrier reproduced a CONFIRMED booking and SUCCEEDED payment whose winning charge was also REFUNDED. Reload the persisted winner after a failed status update before deciding whether this is a duplicate charge. The related A/B-success variant also needs coverage.
- **Omar/Hicham — refund retry identity (P2):** `refunds.service.ts:87` and `:125`. A lost Stripe response or database-write failure leaves no local refund row; retry creates a new metadata.refundId while reusing the same idempotency key. An isolated actual-service probe confirmed identical keys, different parameters, one accepted external refund and zero local refund rows. The stub then rejected retry with idempotency_error under [Stripe's documented parameter-equality rule](https://docs.stripe.com/api/idempotent_requests). This is not a live Stripe call. Persist/reuse stable operation identity and identical retry parameters, then reconcile external success.
- **Omar/Hicham — current-policy acceptance bypass (P2):** `payments.service.ts:46` reuses an existing payment before checking current policy acceptance; retry also omits the check. Root's actual-service inline probe supplied a current acceptance result of false, yet observed zero acceptance checks and a returned existing client secret. Revalidate current acceptance before reuse/retry, or explicitly coordinate an immutable booking-policy snapshot design.
- **Lama — hotel search quote/capacity mismatch (P2):** current `hotels.service.ts:358`. Four guests/one room with a maximum price of 10000 returns fromPrice 5000 when the cheap room holds only one guest and the only four-person room costs 20000. A root actual-service probe reproduced the result with no capacity-valid room within budget. Capacity qualification and quoted price/filter/sort must refer to compatible room selections.
- **Lama — impossible hotel calendar dates (P2):** `hotels/dto/transforms.ts:1`. Root probes showed 2027-02-31 accepted by blocked-range DTO, search DTO and disconnected RoomAvailability schema validation, all with zero validation errors. Use strict shared calendar-date validation across search/availability/blocking. The probe does not claim an actual database write.
- **Omar/Hicham — blog interaction counter races (P2):** `blog/likes.service.ts:46` and `comments.service.ts:92`. Independent inline probes using actual compiled services paused the post-counter increment after child creation, deleted/unliked the child at a zero cached counter, then resumed creation. Both cases ended with zero child rows but cached count 1. Coordinate each row mutation and counter update atomically, or derive counts consistently from source rows. These probes used stubbed models/barriers, not a live database.
- **Omar/Hicham — post deletion/upload race (P2):** `blog/blog.service.ts:213`. An actual compiled BlogService/PostImagesService probe paused parent deletion after image cleanup, uploaded an image, then resumed deletion. It reproduced no remaining post but one orphaned image blob. Coordinate deletion with all child writers so a deleted post cannot acquire surviving images/comments/likes. Normal draft/hidden/image visibility and deactivated-author anonymity looked sound in source review; no broader security certification is implied.

Known pending integrations are recorded separately: production `FlightBookingsModule` still binds BOOKING_PAYMENTS to PendingPaymentsAdapter (`flight-bookings.module.ts:26`), while payment fixtures override it with the real adapter. Thus cancellation refunds and hold-expiry intent cancellation are not connected in the running application; Omar and Ismail must coordinate this binding. Kiki's itinerary flight/hotel searches still bind pending 503 adapters although the standalone search services now exist, so Kiki/Gamila/Lama can coordinate those adapters. PR #32's hotel reservations similarly await Osama. No pending adapter is relabeled as a completed feature, and these findings are not silently fixed during conflict resolution.

### Corrections prepared on this branch

### Design reasoning and source work

Checked the official workbook requirement already preserved in the ownership matrix (Bonus!D3: reset link expires30minutes, single-use, newer request invalidates older, no account-existence leak). Read current UsersService/password/version/CAS/guards/cookies/config/fixture composition. Consulted OWASP forgotten-password guidance, Nodemailer SMTP/message docs and Mongoose transactions. npm registry reported nodemailer10.4.0 compatible with Node>=20; installed it and current @types/nodemailer8.0.2, preserving all existing dependency versions. Installation reported existing unapproved Scarf/Mongo memory-server install scripts; no unrelated install-script approval was added.

Added public forgot-password/reset-password DTOs and Swagger/controller routes, private PasswordReset and PasswordRecoveryLimit schemas, PasswordRecoveryService, and reusable SMTP MailModule/MailService. Approved names appear only with blank optional values/comments in .env.example. No shared User schema/enum/new role changes. Existing UsersService.replacePassword adds an optional transaction session; existing callers continue unchanged. Shared PasswordService/CompliantPassword, same-origin protection, cookie clearing and credentialVersion invalidation are reused rather than duplicated.

Return generic202 before user lookup or SMTP delivery; process known/unknown request persistence alike. Persist only SHA-256 reset hashes and HMAC email/rate keys. Use cryptographic256-bit tokens, database-enforced deadlines, unique latest-request key, TTL cleanup, active-account/epoch checks and atomic proof consumption plus password replacement. A newer request blocks an earlier job/redemption even during hashing. SMTP has verified/required TLS, bounded timeouts, no protocol logging, fixed configured sender and no external file/URL body loading. Only generic delivery errors are logged. A late failure deletes only that proof hash.

Email/reset request rate limits persist across app instances; API IPs use the socket/default Express trust behavior, not arbitrary forwarded headers. A bounded four-worker in-process queue prevents account-existence timing from SMTP. It is not a durable queue: restart can lose queued delivery, so a user requests a fresh link. This limitation is documented rather than claiming delivery from HTTP202. Trusted page URL comes only from approved config; URL fragments hold raw tokens, POST bodies redeem them. No M2 reset page is built. Missing optional mail settings preserve mandatory app startup and uniformly disable recovery; partially invalid SMTP configuration fails safely without credential values.

Added private select:false User.bookingVersion, default0, safe integer in0..MAX_SAFE_INTEGER. Missing/null legacy values are incremented from0; malformed, fractional, negative or exhausted values fail closed. The existing credentialVersion incrementability predicate is parameterized and reused for both counters with unchanged credential checks. UsersService.acquireBookingGate writes ACTIVE/USER and increments bookingVersion in the caller's MongoDB transaction. Normal booking operations do not change credentialVersion or log users out. Both NEW confirmations call this same gate before inventory/status conversion; idempotent already-confirmed replay preserves history/counter. Both exported hasUpcomingConfirmedBookings helpers accept an optional ClientSession, preserving existing callers and allowing one transaction snapshot for the account operation. Real account writes establish the conflict; this is not a no-op timestamp write or in-memory mutex.

Implemented AccountDeactivationModule/Service/Controller as a composition layer, keeping persistence-only UsersModule/AuthModule free of booking dependency cycles. POST /api/users/me/deactivate requires normal-cookie AuthGuard plus USER role and body confirmed:true only. Identity/credential version come exclusively from the established session. Within one transaction: acquire the User gate FIRST; query real flight and hotel eligibility with the SAME ClientSession; reject upcoming Confirmed bookings409 (including future return legs and ongoing hotel stays until UTC checkout); conditionally change the still-current active account to DEACTIVATED and increment its credential epoch once; revoke all existing sessions; delete bound reset/verification proofs. A stale authenticated epoch is rejected rather than rebased after a password reset. On error all writes roll back and cookies stay; dependency failures never count as no bookings. Success204 expires all three host auth cookies through an extracted shared auth helper also reused by the unchanged logout/password flows. Profile/password/content/history are retained.

Observed both race outcomes with real HTTP/signed payment webhooks and controlled scheduling at the actual User gate, separately for flights and hotels: confirmation wins -> reservation CONFIRMED, deactivation409, account ACTIVE, no refund; deactivation wins -> account DEACTIVATED, no new confirmation, late payment refunded via the existing adapter. Counter writes force MongoDB transaction retries before the losing operation decides from current data. Existing already-confirmed history remains replayable. Pending holds do not block deactivation and retain existing expiry/release/compensation behavior. Missing dependencies fail closed. Account deactivation does not introduce provider/room deactivation ordering, hard deletion, account reactivation or M2 UI.

Added account-deactivation.e2e.test.ts using real Auth/Users/FlightBookings/HotelBookings/Payments/Blog/Itineraries/Reviews modules on isolated local MongoDB.
